generated by the permutations ⟨(45)⟩and ⟨(24)⟩.
We see, then, that the generator ⟨(24)⟩arises at the level of the CY by J2 and J4, leaving I and c2,a
invariant. Yet, at the level of the configuration matrix, swapping row 2 and row 4 cannot be resolved
by any column permutation.
A.1.1
Coordinate Symmetries and Quotient Manifolds
While Gconf and GX3 describe automorphisms of the configuration matrix and the intersection ring,
respectively, the explicit construction of non-simply connected CY manifolds requires analyzing sym-
metries at the level of the ambient space coordinates.
For our purposes, we are interested in freely-acting symmetries in CICY manifolds [52–54]. A com-
plete classification of all freely-acting symmetries that descend from linear actions on the projective
ambient space has been completed in [25].23 Crucially, a given CY can be embedded in multiple dif-
ferent products of projective spaces; whether a symmetry action is realized linearly or non-linearly
depends on this specific choice of embedding [25, 53].
As we explained in the main text, such freely-acting symmetries of CY manifolds are useful for
constructing a non-trivial fundamental group by forming quotients (see e.g. [53, 54, 82–84] for work
in that direction). The existence of such quotients of CY manifolds is important for the existence of
Wilson lines that would break the GUT group to the Standard Model.
Following the convention of [26], let xi = [xi,0 : · · · : xi,ni] denote the homogeneous coordinates of
the i-th projective factor Pni in the ambient space A. A discrete symmetry group Γ acts linearly on A.
23There has been less work on freely-acting discrete symmetries of CY manifolds defined as hyper-surfaces in toric
four-folds. The underlying reflexive polytopes were fully classified in [80], where they also identified 16 reflexive polytopes
admitting a freely-acting symmetry. However, the identification of freely-acting discrete symmetries on these spaces was
initiated in [81].
38


## Page 40


An element g ∈Γ acts via a combination of a permutation σ ∈Sm of projective factors and a linear
transformation on the coordinates of each factor. Specifically, the action takes the form:
g · xi = Mi xσ−1(i) ,
(A.15)
where Mi ∈PGL(ni + 1, C) is a non-singular transformation matrix, typically restricted to a diagonal
matrix of roots of unity.
For X3 ⊂A to be invariant under Γ, the zero locus of the defining polynomials {pj(x)}k
j=1 must be
mapped to itself. This means that the ideal generated by the polynomials must be invariant, and the
polynomials transform into linear combinations of themselves of the same multi-degree:
pj(g · x) =
k
X
l=1
Cjl(g) pl(x) ,
(A.16)
where C(g) is an invertible k × k constant matrix forming a representation of the group Γ. Because
g must map equations to equations of the same multi-degree, the permutation σ must leave the
configuration matrix (A.2) invariant (up to column permutations). Therefore, the permutation part of
any valid coordinate symmetry must be an element of the configuration symmetry group: σ ∈Gconf.
Once the invariant coordinate actions are identified, they are classified by their fixed-point structure:
1. Freely Acting Symmetries: If Γ has no fixed points on X3 (i.e., for all non-trivial g ∈Γ and
g · x ̸= x ∀x ∈X3), the symmetry can be used to define a smooth quotient space eX3 = X3/Γ.
This quotient is a CY manifold with fundamental group π1( eX3) ∼= Γ and a reduced Euler
characteristic χ( eX3) = χ(X3)/|Γ|.
2. Non-Freely Acting Symmetries: If fixed points exist, the symmetry cannot be used to quotient the
manifold without introducing singularities. However, these non-freely acting groups frequently
survive as regular symmetries [26], leading to discrete flavor symmetries in the four-dimensional
EFT.
These symmetries can be related to Gconf and GX3 discussed in Appendix A.1. The action of Γ
on the coordinates induces a pullback g∗on the cohomology group H1,1(X3). In the basis of divisors
given by Ji corresponding to the hyperplanes of Pni, the phase matrices Mi act trivially on the divisor
classes, meaning the induced action on the K¨ahler generators is dictated by the permutation σ:
g∗Ji = Jσ(i) .
(A.17)
Consequently, there is a natural group homomorphism projecting the coordinate symmetry group Γ
onto a subgroup of the configuration symmetry group Gconf. It is using this information that we have
updated the database of freely-acting symmetries provided here with the entries from the database of
the CICYs in [24].

Numbers,” Fortsch. Phys. 66 no. 6, (2018) 1800029, arXiv:1602.06303 [hep-th].
[84] A. Constantin, J. Gray, and A. Lukas, “Hodge Numbers for All CICY Quotients,” JHEP 01
(2017) 001, arXiv:1607.01830 [hep-th].
